Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having just a little bit of trouble at work lately. It's probably just me needing to try harder, I know that, but... I work at a meat processing plant, and I keep starting my tasks, like the sanitation steps, or the batch logs, and the end-of-line paperwork... but I somehow just leave out the very last parts of them. 

I feel really awful even admitting that to you, because I know my job completely depends on finishing them correctly, and I really do want to be a good employee. I just... I don't know why I keep dropping the ball at the very end. I hope it's okay that I came in for this? I just really want to figure out how to do it right, if that makes sense.

Um... well, I really try my best not to let that happen. I always start my shift trying really, really hard to stay completely locked in, you know? Because I want to be a reliable part of the team and do a good job. 

But... I guess maybe sometimes, if it's been a really long stretch... things can get a little, um, fuzzy? Like, after a while, the cutting and the weighing and checking all the labels... they kind of start to blur together just a tiny bit. And I might suddenly realize I’ve been staring at the same tray, or looking at my log sheet, without actually taking in what I’m supposed to do next. 

But I think that's just because the shifts are long and I'm trying so hard to be thorough! I always snap right back to it as soon as I notice. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or anything, because I really do give it my all every single day.

Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's just normal tiredness, right? I mean, anyone would get a little tired after standing on the line for hours.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I don't think so! I really try to be careful about what I do. Especially at work—I mean, working with knives and the processing machines, you have to be super careful, right? So I always try to think things through and follow all the safety rules. I'd never want to do anything reckless or dangerous. 

I guess... maybe sometimes if I'm at the store, I might buy a little treat or something I didn't plan on getting? Like a candy bar at the register. But that's just a normal thing people do, isn't it? I don't do anything crazy or make big decisions without thinking. I really try to be responsible and do the right thing. I hope I'm making sense? I just don't want you to think I'm careless or anything.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F43.0, F90.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having just a little bit of trouble at work lately. It's probably just me needing to try harder, I know that, but... I work at a meat processing plant, and I keep starting my tasks, like the sanitation steps, or the batch logs, and the end-of-line paperwork... but I somehow just leave out the very last parts of them. \n\nI feel really awful even admitting that to you, because I know my job completely depends on finishing them correctly, and I really do want to be a good employee. I just... I don't know why I keep dropping the ball at the very end. I hope it's okay that I came in for this? I just really want to figure out how to do it right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous when you're working on these tasks?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so? I mean, I don't feel nervous or anything while I'm actually doing them. I know how to do the steps, and I really do care about doing a good job, so I feel pretty calm in the moment. It's just... I don't know, I just sort of drift off right at the end without realizing it? \n\nI'm sorry, I wish I had a better explanation for you. I don't want it to sound like I'm just not paying attention on purpose, because I really am trying to focus. But... no, I don't feel anxious while I'm working. I hope that's an okay answer? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble finishing tasks or projects in your personal life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess, maybe a little bit? But nothing too bad, really. I mean, I try to keep my apartment nice, and I usually get most of my chores done. Like, I'll start doing the laundry and I'll get it washed and dried... I just sometimes forget to fold it right away, or put it in the drawers. It just sits in the basket for a few days. But I always mean to finish it! \n\nAnd sometimes with hobbies... I started trying to knit a scarf a few months ago, and I got about halfway through, but then I just sort of... stopped picking it up. I feel silly even mentioning it, because everybody has half-finished projects, right? It's probably totally normal. I don't want to make a big deal out of nothing. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I can sit still just fine. I might, you know, bounce my leg a tiny bit sometimes, or maybe play with my pen during a meeting, but nothing crazy. It's just normal fidgeting, I think? Like what anyone does when they've been sitting for a while. I definitely don't feel like I need to get up and run around or anything like that. I try really hard to be respectful and pay attention when I'm supposed to be seated. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I'm not doing anything disruptive, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel uncomfortable or stressed?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe a little? I really don't want to sound like I'm lazy or trying to get out of work, because I actually care a lot about my job at the processing plant. But... I guess I do sort of put off the, um, certification modules? And whenever we have really long procedure reading to do. \n\nI always get them done, I promise! I just... tend to wait until the very last possible minute to start them. I don't really know why, but sitting there trying to read and absorb all those rules and regulations... it just feels almost physically exhausting to get through it all. I feel terrible even admitting that, because I know how important all the safety rules are, and I really do want to do things right. I just struggle to push through those long reading parts. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or anything?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself for not completing tasks or making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I guess I can be a little hard on myself sometimes? But I think that's just because I really want to do a good job, you know? Like at the plant, I want to make sure I'm pulling my weight and doing everything right. \n\nSo if I do mess up, or if I put off those modules like I mentioned... I might feel a little bad about it. But I don't think it's anything extreme. I just... I try to hold myself to a good standard. Everybody feels a little guilty when they make a mistake, right? That's just being conscientious, I think. I definitely don't want you to think I'm sitting around beating myself up all day or anything like that. I'm usually pretty okay! Is that... is that normal?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking you're not good enough?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try to stay pretty positive about myself. I know I have a good degree and I work hard, so I try to focus on that. \n\nI guess sometimes if I'm really struggling to get through those long safety manuals or something, I might have a passing thought like, \"Why is this so hard for me?\" But I think everyone has moments where they doubt themselves a little bit, right? It's not like a constant thing where I'm always thinking I'm not good enough or anything. I just want to do my best and be a good employee. I hope that sounds okay? I promise I'm generally a pretty confident person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. Um... definitely not. I really don't want you to worry about me in that way or anything! I actually think I have a lot to offer. I mean, I worked really hard to get my bachelor's degree, and I'm really proud of that, you know? \n\nI definitely don't feel worthless. Like I was saying before, I might get a little frustrated with myself if I'm dragging my feet on the training modules, but that's just because I care about doing a good job at the plant. I really do value myself and the work I do. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers... but yeah, I promise I don't feel like that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or down for no particular reason?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. Um... I'm actually a pretty upbeat person, I think. I mean, sure, if I have a bad day at work or something, I might feel a little bummed out, but it's always for a reason. It's never just out of nowhere. \n\nI try to keep a smile on my face, especially at the plant. It's a tough environment, you know, processing meat and all, so I think it's important to bring good energy. I definitely don't just sit around feeling sad. I'm usually pretty happy! Is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I just want to be helpful and make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering off to other things when you're in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say it happens *often*, or that it's a big problem or anything. \n\nLike, if I'm doing something really repetitive at the plant, you know, just standing on the line... my mind might drift a tiny bit. But I always catch it right away! I just, you know, think about what I'm going to make for dinner or something normal like that. I really try to stay focused on the meat processing because safety is so important, and I want to be a good worker. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm not paying attention to my job? I promise I'm always trying my best to stay on task. Everyone daydreams a little when they're doing the same motion over and over, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to details when you're working on a task?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, paying attention to details? I think I'm pretty good at that, actually! I mean, I really try to be. \n\nAt the plant, there are a lot of little things you have to watch out for, and I always make sure I'm following the rules. I guess... maybe sometimes if I'm doing the exact same cut for hours, I might miss a tiny thing here or there? But nothing major, I promise! Just normal little slip-ups that anyone could make. I always try to double-check my work because I really want to do a good job for my supervisor. I hope that's an okay answer? I just don't want you to think I'm careless or anything, because I really do care about doing things right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your attention on one task for a long time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try my best not to let that happen. I always start my shift trying really, really hard to stay completely locked in, you know? Because I want to be a reliable part of the team and do a good job. \n\nBut... I guess maybe sometimes, if it's been a really long stretch... things can get a little, um, fuzzy? Like, after a while, the cutting and the weighing and checking all the labels... they kind of start to blur together just a tiny bit. And I might suddenly realize I’ve been staring at the same tray, or looking at my log sheet, without actually taking in what I’m supposed to do next. \n\nBut I think that's just because the shifts are long and I'm trying so hard to be thorough! I always snap right back to it as soon as I notice. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or anything, because I really do give it my all every single day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to focus on your tasks as the day goes on?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think that's just normal tiredness, right? I mean, anyone would get a little tired after standing on the line for hours. \n\nI really try to push through it, though! I drink some coffee on my break and tell myself to just focus harder. Sometimes, toward the end of the shift, it feels like it takes a lot more energy to keep track of the different cuts and make sure I'm doing everything perfectly. Like my brain is just working a little slower? But I never stop trying, I promise. I always want to finish strong so my supervisor knows I'm a hard worker. It's totally normal to get a little worn out by the end of the day, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing? I think I keep things pretty neat! I mean, I really try to. At work, they have a specific way everything is supposed to be set up at your station, and I always try to follow it exactly. I want to make sure I'm doing it the right way.\n\nI guess... well, sometimes my locker gets a little bit messy? Or maybe at home, my room isn't always perfectly tidy. Sometimes things pile up a little bit on my desk or my bed, but I always clean it up eventually! I swear I'm not a messy person, I just... sometimes I get so tired from work that I leave things out for a little bit. But I always know where everything is, mostly! That's okay, right? I mean, nobody's perfectly organized all the time. I just don't want you to think I'm living in a disaster zone or anything, because I really do try to keep things nice.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble controlling your emotions when something frustrating happens?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really! I think I'm usually pretty good at keeping my cool. I mean, I try really hard to be easy to work with. If a machine jams or a delivery is late, I just... take a deep breath, you know? \n\nI don't really get mad or yell or anything like that. I wouldn't want to make anyone else uncomfortable or cause a scene on the floor. Sometimes I might feel a little bit stressed inside if things are going wrong, but I just keep it to myself and try to fix the problem. I think I handle it okay! I mean, everyone gets a little frustrated sometimes, right? But I always try to stay positive and keep a smile on my face. I hope that's the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so! I really try to be careful about what I do. Especially at work—I mean, working with knives and the processing machines, you have to be super careful, right? So I always try to think things through and follow all the safety rules. I'd never want to do anything reckless or dangerous. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes if I'm at the store, I might buy a little treat or something I didn't plan on getting? Like a candy bar at the register. But that's just a normal thing people do, isn't it? I don't do anything crazy or make big decisions without thinking. I really try to be responsible and do the right thing. I hope I'm making sense? I just don't want you to think I'm careless or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing focus in the middle of tasks that require sustained mental effort?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I always try really hard to pay attention! I want to make sure I'm doing a good job and doing everything right. But... I guess maybe with certain things? Like, if I have to do the, um, certification modules for work, or read through any of the really long procedure updates... I might sort of put those off until the last possible minute. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, but I promise I always get them done before the deadline! It's just... sitting there, trying to absorb all those rules and technical stuff... it honestly feels almost physically exhausting to me. Like it takes everything I have just to get through a few pages. \n\nBut I always push through eventually! I mean, a lot of people find that kind of paperwork tiring, right? I just hope it doesn't make me sound lazy or anything, because I really do work hard when I'm actually on the floor processing things. That's normal, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often struggle to motivate yourself to start tasks?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really wouldn't say I struggle with motivation! I mean, I always want to do a good job and be as helpful as I can be. If my supervisor needs me to prep a new station or break down a different cut, I just jump right in and do it. I really care about being a good employee, you know?\n\nI guess... well, like I said before about the training modules, maybe I take a little while to actually open the web page for those? But it's definitely not because I don't *want* to do my job. I always show up for my shifts on time and I'm always ready to work hard. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just sitting around or that I'm not a go-getter. \n\nSo, no, I don't think I have a hard time motivating myself to start things. I really try my best to just get to work right away. That's a good thing, right? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers!\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
